43:0843 minutes, 8 secondssee who is interested in your stuff. And once solved, it reveals another problem solved by your core offer. This is

1:59:431 hour, 59 minutes, 43 secondsaccountants. Celebrity including prominent people, celebrities, authorities, etc. Example. Normally, we

3:18:163 hours, 18 minutes, 16 secondsAnd yes, if you're curious, it got more sophisticated as time went on. How paid ads work. Paid ads are a way to
3:18:233 hours, 18 minutes, 23 secondsadvertise one to many to cold audiences, people who don't know you. Paid ads work by paying another person or business to
3:18:313 hours, 18 minutes, 31 secondsput your offer in front of their audience. Think of it like renting eyeballs or earballs. And because you don't need to spend time building an
3:18:383 hours, 18 minutes, 38 secondsaudience, paid ads are the fastest way to get the most people to see your stuff. You trade money for reach, a considerable advantage when you know
3:18:473 hours, 18 minutes, 47 secondswhat you're doing. Ads are riskier, but when done right, they can get you more leads than any other method. With warm
3:18:543 hours, 18 minutes, 54 secondsand cold outreach, we have to do more stuff to reach more people. To reach more people with free content, we depend on the platform or audience sharing it
3:19:033 hours, 19 minutes, 3 secondsif they feel like it. Paid ads are different. The reach is guaranteed, but getting your money back isn't. So, it's a game of efficiency rather than reach.
3:19:123 hours, 19 minutes, 12 secondsLet me explain. In principle, if you paid enough money, you could get every person in the world to see your ad. And if every person in the world saw your
3:19:203 hours, 19 minutes, 20 secondsad, someone would buy, even if only by accident. How? So, the question isn't, do ads work? It's how well can you make
3:19:283 hours, 19 minutes, 28 secondsthem work? In other words, it's a push and pull between how much you spend and how much they buy. And like cold outreach, paid ads go to cold or lower
3:19:373 hours, 19 minutes, 37 secondstrust audiences. So even with good offers, a smaller percentage of people will respond. And like cold outreach,
3:19:443 hours, 19 minutes, 44 secondspaid ads get over this hurdle by putting your offer in front of more people. And if an ad isn't profitable, most of the time it's because the right people never
3:19:523 hours, 19 minutes, 52 secondssaw it. So to make an ad profitable, the right people have to see it. This keeps our ads efficient. This chapter reveals
3:20:003 hours, 20 minuteshow I create more efficient paid ads by finding needles in the haystack. I start with the entire world as my audience
3:20:083 hours, 20 minutes, 8 secondshaystack, then narrow down to get a higher percentage of engaged leads needles. First, I pick a platform that
3:20:153 hours, 20 minutes, 15 secondscontains my ideal audience. Second, I use whatever targeting methods that exist within the platform to find them.
3:20:223 hours, 20 minutes, 22 secondsThird, I craft my ad in a way that repels anyone else. Finally, I tell whoever's left standing to take the next
3:20:293 hours, 20 minutes, 29 secondsstep. People over complicate it, but that's it. That's all we're doing.
3:20:343 hours, 20 minutes, 34 secondsNarrowing down who sees our ad so we have the highest chance of getting the right type of people to respond. Once we advertise profitably in a small puddle
3:20:423 hours, 20 minutes, 42 secondsof an audience, we expand to a pond, then a lake, than an ocean. And as the audience gets bigger, it does have more
3:20:503 hours, 20 minutes, 50 secondsof the wrong people, but it has more of the right ones, too. So ads decrease in efficiency, but at that point you can
3:20:563 hours, 20 minutes, 56 secondsafford it. In other words, the ratio between what you spend and how much they buy goes down, but the total amount of money you make goes up. So instead of

3:22:443 hours, 22 minutes, 44 secondssend message ads or you can run newsfeed ads. On Instagram, you can run ads to the news feed or stories. On YouTube,

3:37:113 hours, 37 minutes, 11 secondsyour thing? It tells people why they should want a lead magnet or offer. Now, there are a million ways to do this, but I'll share with you my what, who, when
3:37:203 hours, 37 minutes, 20 secondsframework. This mental framework hinges on knowing the value equation forwards and backwards. So, all you have to do is
3:37:283 hours, 37 minutes, 28 secondsknow eight key things about your own product or service. How it fulfills each element of value for your prospect and how it helps them avoid their hidden
3:37:373 hours, 37 minutes, 37 secondscosts. Remember those. Think of them like carrots versus sticks. How your offer delivers more good stuff and less
3:37:443 hours, 37 minutes, 44 secondsbad stuff. Then think of the perspectives of the people who would experience them. Who? And finally, what time period when they'd have these
3:37:533 hours, 37 minutes, 53 secondsexperiences, positive or negative? In the words of David Oglevie, the customer isn't a [ __ ] She's your wife. So, you

3:38:083 hours, 38 minutes, 8 secondsquestions at precisely the time they think it. So, if you can answer what they're thinking with your ad using the words they'd use, you've won. So, let's start with the what. Eight key elements.
3:38:193 hours, 38 minutes, 19 secondsDream outcome. A good ad will show and tell the maximum benefit the prospect can achieve using the thing you sell. It
3:38:273 hours, 38 minutes, 27 secondsshould align with the ideal prospect's dream outcome for that sort of product or service. These are the results they experience after buying the thing.
3:38:353 hours, 38 minutes, 35 secondsOpposite nightmare. A good ad will also show them the worst possible hassles, pain, etc. of going without your
3:38:433 hours, 38 minutes, 43 secondssolution. In short, the bad stuff they'll experience if they don't buy.
3:38:483 hours, 38 minutes, 48 secondsperceived likelihood of achievement because of past failures. We assume that even when we buy, there's a risk we don't get what we want. Lower perceived
3:38:573 hours, 38 minutes, 57 secondsrisk by minimizing or explaining away past failures. Emphasizing the success of people like them. Giving assurances
3:39:043 hours, 39 minutes, 4 secondsby authority, guarantees, and how what you have to offer will at least give them a better chance of success than what they currently do, etc. Opposite
3:39:133 hours, 39 minutes, 13 secondsrisk. A good ad will also show them how risky it is to not act. What will their life be like if they carried on as they
3:39:203 hours, 39 minutes, 20 secondsalways have? Show how they will repeat their past failures and how their problems will get bigger and worse. Time
3:39:273 hours, 39 minutes, 27 secondsdelay. A good ad will also show them how slow their current trajectory is or that they'll never get what they want at
3:39:333 hours, 39 minutes, 33 secondstheir current rate. Opposite speed. To get things we want, we know we have to spend time getting them. A good ad will
3:39:403 hours, 39 minutes, 40 secondsshow and tell how much faster they will get the thing they want. Effort and sacrifice. A good ad will also show them
3:39:483 hours, 39 minutes, 48 secondsthe amount of work and skill they'll need to get the result without your solution and how they'll be forced to keep giving up the things they love and
3:39:563 hours, 39 minutes, 56 secondscontinue suffering from the things they hate. Or worse, that they work hard and sacrifice a ton right now and have
3:40:033 hours, 40 minutes, 3 secondsgotten nowhere. In other words, they waste more time and money doing what they currently do than if they just bought our darn solution. Opposite ease.
3:40:133 hours, 40 minutes, 13 secondsTo get things we want, we know we have to change something, but we then assume we have to do stuff we hate and give up stuff we love. And ease comes from a
3:40:223 hours, 40 minutes, 22 secondslack of needed work or skill. A good ad disproves the assumption. It tells and shows how you can avoid the stuff you
3:40:293 hours, 40 minutes, 29 secondshate doing, do more of the stuff you love doing without working hard or having a lot of skill, and still get the dream outcome. Those are the eight key
3:40:383 hours, 40 minutes, 38 secondselements. Now we fully understand the what, how we deliver the four value elements and how we avoid their four
3:40:453 hours, 40 minutes, 45 secondsopposites. We now go to the next W. The who. Who humans are primarily status driven and the status of one human comes
3:40:533 hours, 40 minutes, 53 secondsfrom how the other humans treat them. So if your product or service changes how other people treat your customer, which it does in some way, it pays to show
3:41:013 hours, 41 minutes, 1 secondhow. And talking about the value elements from someone else's perspective shows all the ways it'll improve the status of your customer. So we want to
3:41:103 hours, 41 minutes, 10 secondsoutline two groups of people. The first group is the people gaining status, your customers. The second group is the
3:41:173 hours, 41 minutes, 17 secondspeople giving it to them. Spouse, kids, parents, extended family, colleagues, bosses, friends, rivals, competitors,
3:41:253 hours, 41 minutes, 25 secondsetc. All of these perspectives give us different opportunities to show how the prospect's status may improve. And they

3:41:423 hours, 41 minutes, 42 secondslikely to get promoted at work? Science says yes. Does your friendmy no longer make those little jabs at dinner? Let's

3:41:573 hours, 41 minutes, 57 secondsrisk. I talk about how their kids would notice they weren't as stressed or distracted anymore about work. How their competitors notice their phones don't

3:42:233 hours, 42 minutes, 23 secondsown perspective. And we can apply each new who perspective to each value driver. This is how you get so many
3:42:293 hours, 42 minutes, 29 secondsdifferent stories, examples, angles, etc. to describe the benefits more carrot and less stick. That leads me to
3:42:373 hours, 42 minutes, 37 secondsthe third lens of the what who win framework, the when. When people often only think of how their decisions affect

3:59:593 hours, 59 minutes, 59 secondsIf that sounds painfully obvious, good.
4:00:024 hours, 2 secondsYou've already got most people beat. I measure paid ad efficiency by comparing the lifetime gross profit of a customer
4:00:094 hours, 9 secondsLTGP with the cost to acquire a customer CAC. I express this ratio as LTGP to
4:00:164 hours, 16 secondsCAC. I measure LTGP instead of lifetime value or LTV. Lifetime gross profit is
4:00:234 hours, 23 secondsall the money a customer ever spends on your stuff minus all the money it takes to deliver it. For example, if a customer buys something for $15 and it
4:00:324 hours, 32 secondscosts $5 to deliver it, your gross profit is $10. So, if that customer buys 10 things over their lifetime, then they
4:00:414 hours, 41 secondsbought a total of $150 in stuff. But it cost you a total of $50 to deliver that stuff. That makes the lifetime gross
4:00:494 hours, 49 secondsprofit $100. Gross profit is important in general because it's the actual money you use to acquire customers, pay rent,
4:00:574 hours, 57 secondscover payroll, and everything else to run your business. So, if you've ever heard me say, "I'm getting 3:1 on this."
4:01:054 hours, 1 minute, 5 secondsI refer my LTGP to CAC ratio. I compare how much I made against how much I spent. So, if LTGP is greater than CAC,
4:01:134 hours, 1 minute, 13 secondsyou have profitable advertising. If it's lower than CAC, you're losing money.
4:01:174 hours, 1 minute, 17 secondsWhat's a good LTGP to CAC ratio? Every business I invest in that struggles to scale has at least one thing in common.
4:01:254 hours, 1 minute, 25 secondsTheir LTGP to CAC ratio was less than 3:1. As soon as I get above 3:1, either
4:01:324 hours, 1 minute, 32 secondsthrough decreasing CAC or increasing LTGP, they take off. This is a pattern I personally observed, not a rule. You have two big levers to improving LTGP.
4:01:434 hours, 1 minute, 43 secondsCAC, make CAC lower, get cheaper customers. We do this with more efficient ads following the steps we just outlined. Make LTGP higher.
4:01:534 hours, 1 minute, 53 secondsIncrease how much you make per customer. We do this with a better business model. For maximum money, I prefer to do both.
4:02:004 hours, 2 minutesFor example, if you made a billion per customer, then you could spend $999 million to get a customer and still have
4:02:084 hours, 2 minutes, 8 secondsa million dollars left over. You could spend pretty much whatever it takes to get a customer. No matter how crappy your ads, you'd still probably win. On

4:02:324 hours, 2 minutes, 32 secondsads, high CAC, when in reality, they have a crappy business model, low LTGP.

4:02:504 hours, 2 minutes, 50 secondsbetween the winners and the losers is how much they make off each customer.
